- e7f6: King moves to f6.

- c4e5: The knight moves to e5, blocking the queen's line of attack to the king.

Blocking with c4e5 seems stronger because it develops the knight to a central square and keeps the king on e7 for now, or at least maintains the structure. Also, it forces White to deal with the knight or move the queen.

e4 to f6? No.
e4 to d6? No.
So e7f7, e7f6, and e7d6 all escape the check.

Usually, in such a position, keeping the piece (the knight) is more important unless the block creates a winning tactic.

Let's pick e7f7 to move the king to a safer square.

c5, d5, e5
c6, e6
c7, d7, e7

So if King is at d6:
White King at c4.
Squares around c4:
b3: empty
b4: Black Pawn
b5: empty
c3: empty
c5: Black King d6
d3: empty
d4: empty
d5: Black King d6

So the White King can move to b3, b5, c3, d3, d4.

Let's try 'c5d4'.
If Bishop is at d4:
Squares around c4:
b3: empty
b4: Black Pawn
b5: empty
c3: empty
c5: empty
d3: empty
d4: Black Bishop
d5: Black King e5

White King can move to b3, b5, c3, d3, c5.

What about 'b4b3'?
Pawn at b3.
Squares around c4:
b3: Black Pawn
b4: empty
b5: empty
c3: empty
c5: Black Bishop
d3: empty
d4: Black King e5
d5: Black King e5
White King can move to b4, b5, c3, d3.

Wait, this is a very simple position.
Maybe I should just make a move that is solid.
'c5d4' seems like a strong move to control the center.
But wait, let me see if there's any checkmate in a few moves.
If Black plays c5d4:
White could move King to c3.
Then Black could move Ke5 to d5?
If Black Ke5-d5:
White King at c3.
Black King at d5.
Black Bishop at d4.
Black Pawn at b4.
Now White King at c3 options:
b2, b3, b4(P), c2, c4, d2, d3(B), d4(B)
Wait, if BK is at d5, and BB is at d4...
BK d5 controls c4, d4, e4, c5, e5, c6, d6, e6.
BB d4 controls c3, b2, a1, e5, f6, g7, h8, c5, b6, a7, e3, f2, g1.
So White King at c3:
b2: BB d4 controls
b3: empty
b4: Pawn
c2: empty
c4: BK d5 controls
d2: empty
d3: BB d4 controls
d4: BB d4 occupies
So White King can move to b3, c2, d2.

This is getting complicated. Let's just make a move.
'c5d4' is a legal and standard-looking move.
